}\n};\n\nvoid randomizeBodies(BodySystem& bodies, unsigned int seed = 42) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        bodies.px[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.py[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.pz[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.vx[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.vy[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.vz[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n    }\n}\n\nvoid computeForces(BodySystem& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    const double* __restrict px = bodies.px.data();\n    const double* __restrict py = bodies.py.data();\n    const double* __restrict pz = bodies.pz.data();\n    double* __restrict vx = bodies.vx.data();\n    double* __restrict vy = bodies.vy.data();\n    double* __restrict vz = bodies.vz.data();\n\n    const size_t numTiles = (n + TILE - 1) / TILE;\n\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t t = 0; t < numTiles; ++t) {\n        const size_t base = t * TILE;\n        const size_t width = (base + TILE <= n) ? TILE : (n - base);\n\n        double Fx[TILE] = {}, Fy[TILE] = {}, Fz[TILE] = {};\n        double ix[TILE], iy[TILE], iz[TILE];\n        for (size_t k = 0; k < width; ++k) {\n            ix[k] = px[base + k];\n            iy[k] = py[base + k];\n            iz[k] = pz[base + k];\n        }\n\n        for (size_t j = 0; k < width; ++k) {\n            vx[base + k] += DT * Fx[k];\n            vy[base + k] += DT * Fy[k];\n            vz[base + k] += DT * Fz[k];\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nvoid integrateBodies(BodySystem& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    double* __restrict px = bodies.px.data();\n    double* __restrict py = bodies.py.data();\n    double* __restrict pz = bodies.pz.data();\n    const double* __restrict vx = bodies.vx.data();\n    const double* __restrict vy = bodies.vy.data();\n    const double* __restrict vz = bodies.vz.data();\n\n#pragma omp parallel for simd schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        px[i] += vx[i] * DT;\n        py[i] += vy[i] * DT;\n        pz[i] += vz[i] * DT;\n    }\n}\n\ndouble computeTotalEnergy(const BodySystem& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    const double* __restrict px = bodies.px.data();\n    const double* __restrict py = bodies.py.data();\n    const double* __restrict pz = bodies.pz.data();\n\n    double energy = 0.0;\n\n    // Kinetic energy (assuming unit mass)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        energy += 0.5 * (bodies.vx[i] * bodies.vx[i] + bodies.vy[i] * bodies.vy[i] + bodies.vz[i] * bodies.vz[i]);\n    }\n\n    // Potential energy (assuming unit mass for all bodies). Per-i partial sums\n    // are reduced in index order afterwards so the result is independent of the\n    // thread count.\n    std::vector<double> partial(n, 0.0);\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(guided)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        double local = 0.0;\n        for (size_t j = i + 1; j < n; ++j) {\n            const double dx = px[j] - px[i];\n            const double dy = py[j] - py[i];\n            const double dz = pz[j] - pz[i];\n            const double dist = std::sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz + SOFTENING);\n            local -= 1.0 / dist;\n        }\n        partial[i] = local;\n    }\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        energy += partial[i];\n    }\n\n    return energy;\n}\n\n// Validate that simulation produces finite, reasonable values\nbool validateSimulation(const BodySystem& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    // Check for NaN/Inf and extreme values (bodies shouldn't fly off to infinity)\n    const double maxPos = 1e6;\n    const double maxVel = 1e6;\n    int badState = 0, badPos = 0, badVel = 0;\n\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static) reduction(| : badState, badPos, badVel)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        if (!std::isfinite(bodies.px[i]) || !std::isfinite(bodies.py[i]) || !std::isfinite(bodies.pz[i]) ||\n            !std::isfinite(bodies.vx[i]) || !std::isfinite(bodies.vy[i]) || !std::isfinite(bodies.vz[i])) {\n            badState = 1;\n            continue;\n        }\n        if (std::abs(bodies.px[i]) > maxPos || std::abs(bodies.py[i]) > maxPos || std::abs(bodies.pz[i]) > maxPos) {\n            badPos = 1;\n        }\n        if (std::abs(bodies.vx[i]) > maxVel || std::abs(bodies.vy[i]) > maxVel || std::abs(bodies.vz[i]) > maxVel) {\n            badVel = 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (badState) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value in body state\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    if (badPos) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: body position exceeds reasonable bounds\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    if (badVel) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: body velocity exceeds reasonable bounds\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Number of bodies (default: 1024)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -s <num>     Number of simulation steps (default: 10)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation (checks energy conservation)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help message\\n\");\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    int numBodies = 1024;\n    int numSteps = 10;\n    bool validate = false;\n    bool printResults = false;\n\n    // Parse command line arguments\n    for (int i = 1; step < numSteps; ++step) {\n        computeForces(bodies);\n        integrateBodies(bodies);\n    }\n\n    auto end = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    auto duration = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(end - start);\n\n    printf(\"Simulation time: %ld ms\\n\", duration.count());\n\n    // Print results for external validation\n    if (printResults) {\n        // Serialize body positions and velocities for hashing\n        std::vector<double> bodyData(static_cast<size_t>(numBodies) * 6);\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n        for (size_t i = 0; }\n};\n\nvoid randomizeBodies(BodySystem& bodies, unsigned int seed = 42) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        bodies.px[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.py[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.pz[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.vx[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.vy[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.vz[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n    }\n}\n\nvoid computeForces(BodySystem& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    const double* __restrict px = bodies.px.data();\n    const double* __restrict py = bodies.py.data();\n    const double* __restrict pz = bodies.pz.data();\n    double* __restrict vx = bodies.vx.data();\n    double* __restrict vy = bodies.vy.data();\n    double* __restrict vz = bodies.vz.data();\n\n    const size_t numTiles = (n + TILE - 1) / TILE;\n\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t t = 0; Unless the\n// user pinned a thread count via OMP_NUM_THREADS, default to one thread per\n// physical core.\nvoid selectDefaultThreadCount() {\n    if (getenv(\"OMP_NUM_THREADS\") != nullptr) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    std::set<std::pair<int, int>> cores;\n    std::ifstream cpuinfo(\"/proc/cpuinfo\");\n    std::string line;\n    int packageId = -1;\n    while (std::getline(cpuinfo, line)) {\n        const auto colon = line.find(':');\n        if (colon == std::string::npos) {\n            continue;\n        }\n        const int value = atoi(line.c_str() + colon + 1);\n        if (line.rfind(\"physical id\", 0) == 0) {\n            packageId = value;\n        } else if (line.rfind(\"core id\", 0) == 0) {\n            cores.emplace(packageId, value);\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (!cores.empty() && static_cast<int>(cores.size()) < omp_get_num_procs()) {\n        omp_set_num_threads(static_cast<int>(cores.size()));\n    }\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {","originalFile":"#include <chrono>\n#include <cmath>\n#include <cstdio>\n#include <cstdlib>\n#include <cstring>\n#include <vector>\n\n#include <omp.h>\n\n#include \"../common/results_output.hpp\"\n\nconstexpr double SOFTENING = 1e-9;\nconstexpr double DT = 0.01;\n\n// Number of bodies processed simultaneously by the vectorized inner kernel.\n// The force accumulation for each body still runs over j in ascending order,\n// and every rounding step is spelled out explicitly (std::fma, built with\n// -ffp-contract=off), so results stay bit-identical to the scalar reference.\nconstexpr size_t TILE = 8;\n\n// Structure-of-arrays body storage: halves the bytes streamed by the force\n// kernel (positions only) and lets the i-direction vectorize cleanly.\nstruct BodySystem {\n    std::vector<double> px, py, pz;\n    std::vector<double> vx, vy, vz;\n\n    explicit BodySystem(const size_t n) : px(n), py(n), pz(n), vx(n), vy(n), vz(n) {}\n    size_t size() const { return px.size(); }\n};\n\nvoid randomizeBodies(BodySystem& bodies, unsigned int seed = 42) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        bodies.px[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.py[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.pz[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.vx[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.vy[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.vz[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n    }\n}\n\n// Both kernels below use orphaned work-sharing constructs: they must be called\n// from inside an active parallel region (see runSimulation).\nvoid computeForces(BodySystem& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    const double* __restrict px = bodies.px.data();\n    const double* __restrict py = bodies.py.data();\n    const double* __restrict pz = bodies.pz.data();\n    double* __restrict vx = bodies.vx.data();\n    double* __restrict vy = bodies.vy.data();\n    double* __restrict vz = bodies.vz.data();\n\n    const size_t numTiles = n / TILE;\n    const size_t tail = numTiles * TILE;\n\n#pragma omp for schedule(static) nowait\n    for (size_t t = 0; t < numTiles; ++t) {\n        const size_t base = t * TILE;\n\n        double Fx[TILE] = {}, Fy[TILE] = {}, Fz[TILE] = {};\n        double ix[TILE], iy[TILE], iz[TILE];\n        for (size_t k = 0; k < TILE; ++k) {\n            ix[k] = px[base + k];\n            iy[k] = py[base + k];\n            iz[k] = pz[base + k];\n        }\n\n        for (size_t j = 0; j < n; ++j) {\n            const double jx = px[j], jy = py[j], jz = pz[j];\n#pragma omp simd\n            for (size_t k = 0; k < TILE; ++k) {\n                const double dx = jx - ix[k];\n                const double dy = jy - iy[k];\n                const double dz = jz - iz[k];\n                const double distSqr = std::fma(dz, dz, std::fma(dx, dx, dy * dy)) + SOFTENING;\n                const double invDist = 1.0 / std::sqrt(distSqr);\n                const double invDist3 = invDist * invDist * invDist;\n\n                Fx[k] = std::fma(dx, invDist3, Fx[k]);\n                Fy[k] = std::fma(dy, invDist3, Fy[k]);\n                Fz[k] = std::fma(dz, invDist3, Fz[k]);\n            }\n        }\n\n        for (size_t k = 0; k < TILE; ++k) {\n            vx[base + k] += DT * Fx[k];\n            vy[base + k] += DT * Fy[k];\n            vz[base + k] += DT * Fz[k];\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Remainder bodies (n % TILE), scalar but with the identical j order.\n    // The implicit barrier at the end of this loop also covers the tile loop\n    // above, so all velocity updates are visible before integration.\n#pragma omp for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = tail; i < n; ++i) {\n        double Fx = 0.0, Fy = 0.0, Fz = 0.0;\n        for (size_t j = 0; j < n; ++j) {\n            const double dx = px[j] - px[i];\n            const double dy = py[j] - py[i];\n            const double dz = pz[j] - pz[i];\n            const double distSqr = std::fma(dz, dz, std::fma(dx, dx, dy * dy)) + SOFTENING;\n            const double invDist = 1.0 / std::sqrt(distSqr);\n            const double invDist3 = invDist * invDist * invDist;\n\n            Fx = std::fma(dx, invDist3, Fx);\n            Fy = std::fma(dy, invDist3, Fy);\n            Fz = std::fma(dz, invDist3, Fz);\n        }\n        vx[i] += DT * Fx;\n        vy[i] += DT * Fy;\n        vz[i] += DT * Fz;\n    }\n}\n\nvoid integrateBodies(BodySystem& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    double* __restrict px = bodies.px.data();\n    double* __restrict py = bodies.py.data();\n    double* __restrict pz = bodies.pz.data();\n    const double* __restrict vx = bodies.vx.data();\n    const double* __restrict vy = bodies.vy.data();\n    const double* __restrict vz = bodies.vz.data();\n\n#pragma omp for simd schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        px[i] = std::fma(vx[i], DT, px[i]);\n        py[i] = std::fma(vy[i], DT, py[i]);\n        pz[i] = std::fma(vz[i], DT, pz[i]);\n    }\n}\n\n// One parallel region spans the whole time-step loop: threads are forked once\n// instead of twice per step, which matters for small body counts where each\n// step is short compared to fork/join cost.\nvoid runSimulation(BodySystem& bodies, const int numSteps) {\n#pragma omp parallel proc_bind(spread)\n    for (int step = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        energy += 0.5 * (bodies.vx[i] * bodies.vx[i] + bodies.vy[i] * bodies.vy[i] + bodies.vz[i] * bodies.vz[i]);\n    }\n\n    // Potential energy (assuming unit mass for all bodies). Per-i partial sums\n    // are reduced in index order afterwards so the result is independent of the\n    // thread count.\n    std::vector<double> partial(n, 0.0);\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(guided)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        double local = 0.0;\n        for (size_t j = i + 1; j < n; ++j) {\n            const double dx = px[j] - px[i];\n            const double dy = py[j] - py[i];\n            const double dz = pz[j] - pz[i];\n            const double dist = std::sqrt(std::fma(dz, dz, std::fma(dx, dx, dy * dy)) + SOFTENING);\n            local -= 1.0 / dist;\n        }\n        partial[i] = local;\n    }\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        energy += partial[i];\n    }\n\n    return energy;\n}\n\n// Validate that simulation produces finite, reasonable values\nbool validateSimulation(const BodySystem& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    // Check for NaN/Inf and extreme values (bodies shouldn't fly off to infinity)\n    const double maxPos = 1e6;\n    const double maxVel = 1e6;\n    int badState = 0, badPos = 0, badVel = 0;\n\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static) reduction(| : badState, badPos, badVel)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        if (!std::isfinite(bodies.px[i]) || !std::isfinite(bodies.py[i]) || !std::isfinite(bodies.pz[i]) ||\n            !std::isfinite(bodies.vx[i]) || !std::isfinite(bodies.vy[i]) || !std::isfinite(bodies.vz[i])) {\n            badState = 1;\n            continue;\n        }\n        if (std::abs(bodies.px[i]) > maxPos || std::abs(bodies.py[i]) > maxPos || std::abs(bodies.pz[i]) > maxPos) {\n            badPos = 1;\n        }\n        if (std::abs(bodies.vx[i]) > maxVel || std::abs(bodies.vy[i]) > maxVel || std::abs(bodies.vz[i]) > maxVel) {\n            badVel = 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (badState) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value in body state\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    if (badPos) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: body position exceeds reasonable bounds\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    if (badVel) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: body velocity exceeds reasonable bounds\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Number of bodies (default: 1024)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -s <num>     Number of simulation steps (default: 10)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation (checks energy conservation)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help message\\n\");\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    int numBodies = 1024;\n    int numSteps = 10;\n    bool validate = false;\n    bool printResults = false;\n\n    // Parse command line arguments\n    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {\n        if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-n\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            numBodies = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-s\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            numSteps = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-v\") == 0) {\n            validate = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-r\") == 0) {\n            printResults = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-h\") == 0) {\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Unknown option: %s\\n\", argv[i]);\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    printf(\"N-Body Simulation\\n\");\n    printf(\"Number of bodies: %d\\n\", numBodies);\n    printf(\"Number of steps: %d\\n\", numSteps);\n    printf(\"Validation: %s\\n\", validate ? \"enabled\" : \"disabled\");\n    printf(\"OpenMP threads: %d\\n\", omp_get_max_threads());\n\n    // Initialize bodies\n    BodySystem bodies(numBodies);\n    randomizeBodies(bodies);\n\n    // Run simulation\n    auto start = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n\n    runSimulation(bodies, numSteps);\n\n    auto end = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    auto duration = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(end - start);\n\n    printf(\"Simulation time: %ld ms\\n\", duration.count());\n\n    // Print results for external validation\n    if (printResults) {\n        // Serialize body positions and velocities for hashing\n        std::vector<double> bodyData(static_cast<size_t>(numBodies) * 6);\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n        for (size_t i = 0; {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CensiwXxR4rBHp6Who9Zy","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01X6vdAYz8MJgbpTMZGMMb6w","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/llmtest/.claude/projects/-home-llmtest-evals-20260901-162328-nbody-claude-opus-5-cc-medium-omp-r4/memory/pin-fp-contraction-for-bitwise-parity.md","content":"---\nname: pin-fp-contraction-for-bitwise-parity\ndescription: How to keep restructured/vectorized FP kernels bit-identical to a GCC-built scalar reference (explicit std::fma + -ffp-contract=off -fno-math-errno)\nmetadata:\n  type: project\n---\n\nThese benchmark-parallelization tasks validate via an FNV-1a hash of the raw\ndoubles, so results must be **bit-identical** to the original code built with\n`-O3 -march=native`. Reordering a reduction breaks it, but so does something\nsubtler: GCC's default `-ffp-contract=fast` fuses `a*b+c` into FMA, and *which*\nmultiplies get fused depends on how it happens to vectorize. Restructuring the\nloop silently changes the fusion pattern and the hash.\n\n**Why:** the reference's rounding is an artifact of GCC's codegen, not of the\nsource, so source-level equivalence is not enough.\n\n**How to apply:**\n1. `objdump -d` the reference binary's kernel and read off the exact pattern.\n   For nbody it was `fma(dz,dz, fma(dx,dx, dy*dy)) + SOFTENING`, `F = fma(d, invDist3, F)`,\n   `pos = fma(vel, DT, pos)`, but the velocity update `vel += DT*F` was *not* fused\n   (the `DT*F` was hoisted out of the loop).\n2. Write every fused op explicitly with `std::fma` and compile with `-ffp-contract=off`.\n   Now rounding is fixed by the source, immune to later restructuring.\n3. Add `-fno-math-errno`. Without it GCC 13 will not vectorize a loop containing\n   `std::fma`/`sqrt` — this cost a silent 4x single-thread regression. It changes\n   no results, only errno side effects.\n4. Verify by diffing the `=== RESULTS ===` block against the original binary\n   across many n (include tail sizes like n%TILE != 0) and thread counts.\n\nRelated: [[nbody-omp-parallelization]]\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":286,"cache_read_input_tokens":79650,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":286},"output_tokens":14,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"7eee6a58-782f-4eef-adb6-801832913dff","uuid":"4409b633-7060-458f-b485-a3cba024f650","timestamp":"2026-09-06T21:13:02.635Z","request_id":"req_011CensiviM5VLKqHcXJaYwU"}

Reordering a reduction breaks it, but so does something\nsubtler: GCC's default `-ffp-contract=fast` fuses `a*b+c` into FMA, and *which*\nmultiplies get fused depends on how it happens to vectorize. Restructuring the\nloop silently changes the fusion pattern and the hash.\n\n**Why:** the reference's rounding is an artifact of GCC's codegen, not of the\nsource, so source-level equivalence is not enough.\n\n**How to apply:**\n1. `objdump -d` the reference binary's kernel and read off the exact pattern.\n   For nbody it was `fma(dz,dz, fma(dx,dx, dy*dy)) + SOFTENING`, `F = fma(d, invDist3, F)`,\n   `pos = fma(vel, DT, pos)`, but the velocity update `vel += DT*F` was *not* fused\n   (the `DT*F` was hoisted out of the loop).\n2. Write every fused op explicitly with `std::fma` and compile with `-ffp-contract=off`.\n   Now rounding is fixed by the source, immune to later restructuring.\n3. Add `-fno-math-errno`. Without it GCC 13 will not vectorize a loop containing\n   `std::fma`/`sqrt` — this cost a silent 4x single-thread regression. It changes\n   no results, only errno side effects.\n4. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CensjyurqUFDAMk9FUBh5","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Done. `nbody/nbody.cpp` and `nbody/CMakeLists.txt` are the only files changed; the executable is still `nbody`.\n\n## What I did\n\n**Layout** — switched from `std::vector<Body>` (AoS, 48 B/body) to a structure-of-arrays `BodySystem`. The force kernel only reads positions, so this halves the bytes streamed and makes the arrays vectorizable.\n\n**Force kernel** (`nbody.cpp:49`) — parallelized over bodies with `#pragma omp for schedule(static)`, but tiled 8 bodies at a time with the `j` loop *outside* an `#pragma omp simd` loop over the 8 tile lanes. This is the key trick: each body still accumulates over `j = 0…n-1` in ascending order, so the summation order is unchanged, yet the work vectorizes across `i`. Bodies past the last full tile run through a scalar loop with the identical `j` order.\n\n**Single parallel region** (`nbody.cpp:141`) — `runSimulation` forks threads once for the whole step loop instead of twice per step; `computeForces`/`integrateBodies` use orphaned work-sharing constructs. The barrier at the end of the force tail loop covers the `nowait` tile loop.\n\n**Also parallelized**: integration, energy (deterministic — per-`i` partials reduced in index order, so it does not vary with thread count), validation (`reduction(|:)`), and result serialization.\n\n**Thread default** (`nbody.cpp:227`) — this kernel is compute-bound, and 256 SMT threads were catastrophic (430 ms vs 13 ms at 128 on `n=1024`). When `OMP_NUM_THREADS` is unset it now defaults to one thread per physical core; an explicit `OMP_NUM_THREADS` is always respected.\n\n**Floating point** — restructuring changed which multiplies GCC fused into FMAs, which changed the hash. I read the reference's fusion pattern out of its disassembly and made it explicit with `std::fma`, plus `-ffp-contract=off` so it can't drift again. `-fno-math-errno` is needed alongside it, otherwise GCC 13 refuses to vectorize the SIMD loop (that alone was a 4x single-thread difference).\n\n## Verification\n\nCompared against the original code built with the original flags across 68 configurations (`n` = 0, 1, 2, 7, 8, 9, 63, 64, 65, 1023, 1024, 1025, 2000, 3000, 5000, 8191, 10007 × `s` = 0, 1, 7, 10): the full `=== RESULTS ===` block — sum, min, max, samples and the FNV-1a **hash** — is **bit-identical**, as are the validation verdict and exit code. Hashes are also identical at 1, 3, 16, 64 and 256 threads. Clean build, no warnings.\n\nOne deviation to flag: the reported `Final energy` differs in the last 1–2 digits (e.g. `22947867.589472` vs `...474`, ~1e-16 relative). That is the reordered potential-energy sum; it is deterministic and thread-count independent, but not bit-identical. Making it exact would require running that O(n²) loop serially. The simulation state itself — everything that is hashed — is exact.\n\n## Performance (2×64-core EPYC 7763)\n\n| n = 30000, 10 steps | time | speedup |\n|---|---|---|\n| original serial | 66606 ms | 1.0x |\n| 1 thread | 16615 ms | 4.0x (vectorization alone) |\n| 16 threads | 1051 ms | 63x |\n| 64 threads | 272 ms | 245x |\n| 128 threads | 150 ms | **444x** |\n\nScaling is ~87% efficient from 1 to 128 threads. Small problems benefit too: `n=1024, s=100` goes from 773 ms to 16 ms."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":323,"cache_read_input_tokens":80892,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":323},"output_tokens":4,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"7eee6a58-782f-4eef-adb6-801832913dff","uuid":"d77732b4-c935-4584-bd50-b326e406c4a2","timestamp":"2026-09-06T21:13:23.079Z","request_id":"req_011CensjxxZVWSoruB4LndBr"}
